1→"""Auto-clustering algorithm — groups issues into task clusters."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.base.config import DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
     6→from desloppify.engine._plan.constants import AUTO_PREFIX
     7→from desloppify.engine._plan.auto_cluster_sync import (
     8→    prune_stale_clusters as _prune_stale_clusters,
     9→    sync_issue_clusters as _sync_issue_clusters,
    10→    sync_subjective_clusters as _sync_subjective_clusters,
    11→)
    12→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel, ensure_plan_defaults
    13→from desloppify.engine._plan.subjective_policy import SubjectiveVisibility
    14→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel, utc_now
    15→
    16→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    17→# Repair
    18→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    19→
    20→def _repair_ghost_cluster_refs(plan: PlanModel, now: str) -> int:
    21→    """Clear override cluster refs that point to non-existent clusters."""
    22→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    23→    overrides = plan.get("overrides", {})
    24→    repaired = 0
    25→    for override in overrides.values():
    26→        cluster_name = override.get("cluster")
    27→        if cluster_name and cluster_name not in clusters:
    28→            override["cluster"] = None
    29→            override["updated_at"] = now
    30→            repaired += 1
    31→    return repaired
    32→
    33→
    34→def auto_cluster_issues(
    35→    plan: PlanModel,
    36→    state: StateModel,
    37→    *,
    38→    target_strict: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE,
    39→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None,
    40→    cycle_just_completed: bool = False,
    41→) -> int:
    42→    """Regenerate auto-clusters from current open issues.
    43→
    44→    Returns count of changes made (clusters created, updated, or deleted).
    45→    """
    46→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    47→
    48→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    49→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    50→
    51→    # Map existing auto-clusters by cluster_key
    52→    existing_by_key: dict[str, str] = {}  # cluster_key → cluster_name
    53→    for name, cluster in list(clusters.items()):
    54→        if cluster.get("auto"):
    55→            ck = cluster.get("cluster_key", "")
    56→            if ck:
    57→                existing_by_key[ck] = name
    58→
    59→    now = utc_now()
    60→    active_auto_keys: set[str] = set()
    61→    changes = 0
    62→
    63→    changes += _sync_issue_clusters(
    64→        plan, issues, clusters, existing_by_key, active_auto_keys, now,
    65→    )
    66→    changes += _sync_subjective_clusters(
    67→        plan, state, issues, clusters, existing_by_key, active_auto_keys, now,
    68→        target_strict=target_strict,
    69→        policy=policy,
    70→        cycle_just_completed=cycle_just_completed,
    71→    )
    72→    changes += _prune_stale_clusters(
    73→        plan, issues, clusters, active_auto_keys, now,
    74→    )
    75→    changes += _repair_ghost_cluster_refs(plan, now)
    76→
    77→    plan["updated"] = now
    78→    return changes
    79→
    80→
    81→__all__ = [
    82→    "AUTO_PREFIX",
    83→    "auto_cluster_issues",
    84→]
    85→

    60→        try:
    61→            return int(raw_index)
    62→        except (TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
    63→            logger.debug("Invalid workflow stage index %r: %s", raw_index, exc)
    64→    return _TRIAGE_STAGE_ORDER.get(workflow_stage_name(item).lower(), 0)
    65→
    66→
    67→def enrich_with_impact(
    68→    items: list[WorkQueueItem], dimension_scores: dict[str, Any]
    69→) -> None:
    70→    """Stamp ``estimated_impact`` on each item based on dimension-level headroom.
    71→
    72→    Impact = ``overall_per_point * headroom`` where headroom = ``100 - score``.
    73→    Items in dimensions with more score headroom sort first.
    74→    """
    75→    if not dimension_scores:
    76→        for item in items:
    77→            item["estimated_impact"] = 0.0
    78→        return
    79→
    80→    breakdown = compute_health_breakdown(dimension_scores)
    81→    entries = breakdown.get("entries", [])
    82→
    83→    # Build lookup: normalized dimension name -> {per_point, headroom}
    84→    dim_impact: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
    85→    for entry in entries:
    86→        name = str(entry.get("name", "")).strip()
    87→        if not name:
    88→            continue
    89→        per_point = float(entry.get("overall_per_point", 0.0))
    90→        score = float(entry.get("score", 0.0))
    91→        headroom = 100.0 - score
    92→        dim_impact[name.lower()] = {"per_point": per_point, "headroom": headroom}
    93→
    94→    for item in items:
    95→        impact = _compute_item_impact(item, dim_impact, get_dimension_for_detector)
    96→        item["estimated_impact"] = impact
    97→
    98→
    99→def _compute_item_impact(
   100→    item: WorkQueueItem,
   101→    dim_impact: dict[str, dict[str, float]],
   102→    get_dimension_for_detector,
   103→) -> float:
   104→    """Compute impact value for a single queue item."""
   105→    kind = item.get("kind", "issue")
   106→
   107→    # Subjective items (synthetic dimensions or subjective issues):
   108→    # look up by detail.dimension_name
   109→    if kind == "subjective_dimension" or item.get("is_subjective"):
   110→        dim_name = detail_dict(item).get("dimension_name", "")
   111→        entry = dim_impact.get(dim_name.lower())
   112→        if entry:
   113→            return entry["per_point"] * entry["headroom"]
   114→        return 0.0
   115→
   116→    # Mechanical issues: use detector -> dimension mapping
   117→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
   118→    if detector:
   119→        dimension = get_dimension_for_detector(detector)
   120→        if dimension:
   121→            entry = dim_impact.get(dimension.name.lower())
   122→            if entry:
   123→                return entry["per_point"] * entry["headroom"]
   124→
   125→    return 0.0
   126→
   127→
   128→def build_issue_items(
   129→    state: StateModel,
   130→    *,
   131→    scan_path: str | None,
   132→    status_filter: str,
   133→    scope: str | None,
   134→    chronic: bool,
   135→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   136→    scoped = path_scoped_issues(state.get("issues", {}), scan_path)
   137→    subjective_scores = subjective_strict_scores(state)
   138→    out: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
   139→
   140→    for issue_id, issue in scoped.items():
   141→        if issue.get("suppressed"):
   142→            continue
   143→        if not status_matches(issue.get("status", "open"), status_filter):
   144→            continue
   145→        if chronic and not (
   146→            issue.get("status") == "open" and issue.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2
   147→        ):
   148→            continue
   149→
   150→        # Evidence-only: skip issues below standalone confidence threshold
   151→        detector = issue.get("detector", "")
   152→        meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   153→        if meta and meta.standalone_threshold:
   154→            threshold_rank = CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(meta.standalone_threshold, 9)
   155→            issue_rank = CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(issue.get("confidence", "low"), 9)
   156→            if issue_rank > threshold_rank:
   157→                continue
   158→
   159→        item = cast(WorkQueueItem, dict(issue))
   160→        item["id"] = issue_id
   161→        item["kind"] = "issue"
   162→        item["is_review"] = is_review_issue(item)
   163→        item["is_subjective"] = is_subjective_issue(item)
   164→        item["review_weight"] = (
   165→            review_issue_weight(item) if item["is_review"] else None
   166→        )
   167→        subjective_score = None
   168→        if item["is_subjective"]:
   169→            detail = detail_dict(issue)
   170→            dim_name = detail.get("dimension_name", "")
   171→            dim_key = detail.get("dimension", "") or slugify(dim_name)
   172→            subjective_score = subjective_scores.get(
   173→                dim_key, subjective_scores.get(dim_name.lower(), 100.0)
   174→            )
   175→        item["subjective_score"] = subjective_score
   176→        supported_fixers = supported_fixers_for_item(state, item)
   177→        item["primary_command"] = primary_command_for_issue(
   178→            item,
   179→            supported_fixers=supported_fixers,
   180→        )
   181→
   182→        if not scope_matches(item, scope):
   183→            continue
   184→        out.append(item)
   185→
   186→    return out
   187→
   188→
   189→def _natural_sort_key(item: WorkQueueItem) -> tuple:
   190→    """Compute natural (non-plan-aware) ranking for queue items."""
   191→    kind = item.get("kind", "issue")
   192→
   193→    # Initial-review subjective items: highest priority
   194→    if kind == "subjective_dimension" and item.get("initial_review"):
   195→        return (_RANK_INITIAL_REVIEW, 0, subjective_score_value(item), item.get("id", ""))
   196→
   197→    # Triage stage items: stage order, blocked after unblocked
   198→    if kind == "workflow_stage":
   199→        blocked_penalty = 1 if item.get("is_blocked") else 0
   200→        stage_index = _workflow_stage_index(item)
   201→        return (_RANK_TRIAGE_STAGE, blocked_penalty, stage_index, item.get("id", ""))
   202→
   203→    # Workflow action items (e.g. create-plan)
   204→    if kind == "workflow_action":
   205→        return (_RANK_WORKFLOW, 0, 0, item.get("id", ""))
   206→
   207→    if kind == "cluster":
   208→        # Clusters sort before individual issues, ordered by action type
   209→        action_pri = ACTION_TYPE_PRIORITY.get(
   210→            item.get("action_type", "manual_fix"), 3
   211→        )
   212→        return (
   213→            _RANK_CLUSTER,
   214→            action_pri,
   215→            -int(item.get("member_count", 0)),
   216→            item.get("id", ""),
   217→        )
   218→
   219→    impact = item.get("estimated_impact", 0.0)
   220→
   221→    if kind == "subjective_dimension" or item.get("is_subjective"):
   222→        return (
   223→            _RANK_ISSUE,
   224→            -impact,
   225→            subjective_score_value(item),
   226→            item.get("id", ""),
   227→        )
   228→
   229→    detail = detail_dict(item)
   230→    review_weight = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
   231→    return (
   232→        _RANK_ISSUE,
   233→        -impact,
   234→        CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(item.get("confidence", "low"), 9),
   235→        -review_weight,
   236→        -int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   237→        item.get("id", ""),
   238→    )
   239→
   240→
   241→def item_sort_key(item: WorkQueueItem) -> tuple:
   242→    """Unified sort key: plan position first, then natural ranking.
   243→
   244→    When ``_plan_position`` is stamped (by :func:`stamp_plan_sort_keys`),
   245→    planned items sort first in plan order, then existing items by natural
   246→    ranking, then newly-discovered items by natural ranking.
   247→
   248→    When no plan fields are stamped, falls back to pure natural ranking.
   249→    """
   250→    plan_pos = item.get("_plan_position")
   251→
   252→    if plan_pos is not None:
   253→        kind = item.get("kind", "issue")
   254→        # Triage stages: even when explicitly planned, maintain the
   255→        # blocked-before-unblocked invariant for correctness.
   256→        if kind == "workflow_stage":
   257→            blocked = 1 if item.get("is_blocked") else 0
   258→            stage_idx = _workflow_stage_index(item)
   259→            return (_TIER_PLANNED, blocked, stage_idx, item.get("id", ""))
   260→        return (_TIER_PLANNED, 0, plan_pos, item.get("id", ""))
   261→
   262→    is_new = item.get("_is_new", False)
   263→    group = _TIER_NEW if is_new else _TIER_EXISTING
   264→    return (group, *_natural_sort_key(item))
   265→
   266→
   267→__all__ = [
   268→    "build_issue_items",
   269→    "enrich_with_impact",
   270→    "item_explain",
   271→    "item_sort_key",
   272→    "subjective_score_value",
   273→    "group_queue_items",
   274→]
   275→

1→"""TypeScript function-level smell detectors — monster functions, stub functions, etc.
     2→
     3→Every public detector has the uniform signature ``(ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts) → None``.

     5→

    36→
    37→_MONSTER_FUNCTION_LOC = 150
    38→_HIGH_CYCLOMATIC_THRESHOLD = 15
    39→_NESTED_CLOSURE_THRESHOLD = 3
    40→_CATCH_DEFAULT_FIELD_THRESHOLD = 2
    41→_SWITCH_CASE_MINIMUM = 2
    42→
    43→_MAX_CATCH_BODY = 1000  # max characters to scan for catch block body
    44→_MAX_SWITCH_BODY_SCAN = 5000
    45→
    46→# Basenames that indicate logger/error-handler utility files (case-insensitive).
    47→_ERROR_HANDLER_BASENAMES = ("logger", "errorpresentation", "errorhandler", "errorreporting")
    48→
    49→# Patterns in preceding context that indicate legitimate error handling.
    50→_PRECEDING_SKIP_PATTERNS = re.compile(
    51→    r"componentDidCatch|import\.meta\.env\.DEV|process\.env\.NODE_ENV"
    52→)
    53→
    54→# Patterns in following lines that count as "handled" (not just throw/return).
    55→_HANDLED_RE = re.compile(
    56→    r"\b(?:throw|return)\b|toast\(|normalizeAndPresentError\(|presentError\(|rethrow"
    57→)
    58→
    59→_FUNC_RE = re.compile(r"\bfunction\s*[\w(]")
    60→_ARROW_RE = re.compile(r"=>\s*\{")
    61→
    62→_TS_BRANCH_PATTERNS = (
    63→    re.compile(r"\bif\s*\("),
    64→    re.compile(r"\belse\s+if\s*\("),
    65→    re.compile(r"\bcase\s+"),
    66→    re.compile(r"\bcatch\s*\("),
    67→    re.compile(r"\bfor\s*\("),
    68→    re.compile(r"\bwhile\s*\("),
    69→)
    70→
    71→_OPERATOR_BRANCH_RE = re.compile(r"&&|\|\||\?(?!=)")
    72→
    73→# ── Empty-if-chain patterns ─────────────────────────────────
    74→
    75→_IF_START = re.compile(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\(")
    76→_SINGLE_EMPTY_IF = re.compile(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*\}\s*$")
    77→_SINGLE_EMPTY_ELSE_IF = re.compile(r"else\s+if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*\}\s*$")
    78→_SINGLE_EMPTY_ELSE = re.compile(r"(?:\}\s*)?else\s*\{\s*\}\s*$")
    79→_MULTI_IF_OPEN = re.compile(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$")
    80→_MULTI_ELSE_IF_OPEN = re.compile(r"\}\s*else\s+if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$")
    81→_MULTI_ELSE_OPEN = re.compile(r"\}\s*else\s*\{\s*$")
    82→_ELSE_CONT = re.compile(r"else\s")
    83→
    84→# ── Internal helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────
    85→
    86→
    87→def _emit(
    88→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]],
    89→    key: str,
    90→    ctx: _FileContext,
    91→    line: int,
    92→    content: str,
    93→) -> None:
    94→    """Append a smell issue."""
    95→    smell_counts[key].append({"file": ctx.filepath, "line": line, "content": content})
    96→
    97→
    98→def _find_function_start(line: str, next_lines: list[str]) -> str | None:
    99→    """Return function name for declarations/assignments, else None."""
   100→    stripped = line.strip()
   101→    if not stripped:
   102→        return None
   103→    if stripped.startswith(("interface ", "type ", "enum ", "class ")):
   104→        return None
   105→
   106→    declaration_match = re.match(
   107→        r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:default\s+)?(?:async\s+)?function\s+([A-Za-z_]\w*)\s*\(",
   108→        stripped,
   109→    )
   110→    if declaration_match:
   111→        return declaration_match.group(1)
   112→
   113→    assignment_match = re.match(
   114→        r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:const|let|var)\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)\b",
   115→        stripped,
   116→    )
   117→    if not assignment_match:
   118→        return None
   119→
   120→    combined = "\n".join(
   121→        [stripped] + [next_line.strip() for next_line in next_lines[:2]]
   122→    )
   123→    eq_pos = combined.find("=", assignment_match.end())
   124→    if eq_pos == -1:
   125→        return None
   126→    after_eq = combined[eq_pos + 1 :].lstrip()
   127→    if re.match(r"(?:async\s+)?\([^)]*\)\s*=>", after_eq):
   128→        return assignment_match.group(1)
   129→    if re.match(r"function\b", after_eq):
   130→        return assignment_match.group(1)
   131→    return None
   132→
   133→
   134→def _find_opening_brace_line(lines: list[str], start: int, *, window: int = 5) -> int | None:
   135→    for idx in range(start, min(start + window, len(lines))):
   136→        if "{" in lines[idx]:
   137→            return idx
   138→    return None
   139→
   140→
   141→def _extract_function_body(
   142→    lines: list[str], start_line: int, *, max_scan: int = 2000,
   143→) -> str | None:
   144→    """Extract the inner body text of a function starting at start_line.
   145→
   146→    Returns the text between the opening and closing braces, or None.
   147→    """
   148→    brace_line = _find_opening_brace_line(lines, start_line, window=5)
   149→    if brace_line is None:
   150→        return None
   151→    end_line = _track_brace_body(lines, brace_line, max_scan=max_scan)
   152→    if end_line is None:
   153→        return None
   154→    body_text = "\n".join(lines[brace_line : end_line + 1])
   155→    first_brace = body_text.find("{")
   156→    last_brace = body_text.rfind("}")
   157→    if first_brace == -1 or last_brace == -1 or first_brace >= last_brace:
   158→        return None
   159→    return body_text[first_brace + 1 : last_brace]
   160→
   161→
   162→def _count_pattern_in_body(body: str, pattern: re.Pattern[str]) -> int:
   163→    """Count regex matches in body that aren't inside string literals."""
   164→    return len(pattern.findall(_code_text(body)))
   165→
   166→
   167→def _compute_ts_cyclomatic_complexity(body: str) -> int:
   168→    """Compute cyclomatic complexity for a TypeScript function body string."""
   169→    stripped = _strip_ts_comments(body)
   170→    complexity = 1
   171→    for pattern in _TS_BRANCH_PATTERNS:
   172→        complexity += len(pattern.findall(stripped))
   173→    complexity += len(_OPERATOR_BRANCH_RE.findall(stripped))
   174→    return complexity
   175→
   176→
   177→# ── Detectors (alphabetical — uniform signature: ctx, smell_counts) ──
   178→
   179→
   180→def _detect_async_no_await(
   181→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   182→):
   183→    """Find async functions that don't use await."""
   184→    async_re = re.compile(r"(?:async\s+function\s+(\w+)|(\w+)\s*=\s*async)")
   185→    for i, line in enumerate(ctx.lines):
   186→        m = async_re.search(line)
   187→        if not m:
   188→            continue
   189→        name = m.group(1) or m.group(2)
   190→        body = _extract_function_body(ctx.lines, i)
   191→        if body is not None and not re.search(r"\bawait\b", _code_text(body)):
   192→            _emit(
   193→                smell_counts, "async_no_await", ctx, i + 1,
   194→                f"async {name or '(anonymous)'} has no await",
   195→            )
   196→
   197→
   198→def _detect_catch_return_default(
   199→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   200→):
   201→    """Find catch blocks that return object literals with default/no-op values.
   202→
   203→    Catches the pattern:
   204→      catch (...) { ... return { key: false, key: null, key: () => {} }; }
   205→
   206→    This is a silent failure — the caller gets valid-looking data but the
   207→    operation actually failed.
   208→    """
   209→    catch_re = re.compile(r"catch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{")
   210→    for m in catch_re.finditer(ctx.content):
   211→        body = _extract_block_body(ctx.content, m.end() - 1, _MAX_CATCH_BODY)
   212→        if body is None:
   213→            continue
   214→
   215→        # Check if body contains "return {" — a return with object literal
   216→        return_obj = re.search(r"\breturn\s*\{", body)
   217→        if not return_obj:
   218→            continue
   219→
   220→        # Extract the returned object content
   221→        obj_start = body.find("{", return_obj.start())
   222→        obj_content = _extract_block_body(body, obj_start)
   223→        if obj_content is None:
   224→            continue
   225→
   226→        # Count default/no-op fields
   227→        noop_count = len(re.findall(r"\(\)\s*=>\s*\{\s*\}", obj_content))  # () => {}
   228→        false_count = len(
   229→            re.findall(r":\s*(?:false|null|undefined|0|''|\"\")\b", obj_content)
   230→        )
   231→        default_fields = noop_count + false_count
   232→
   233→        if default_fields >= _CATCH_DEFAULT_FIELD_THRESHOLD:
   234→            line_no, snippet = _content_line_info(ctx.content, m.start())
   235→            _emit(smell_counts, "catch_return_default", ctx, line_no, snippet)
   236→
   237→
   238→def _detect_dead_useeffects(
   239→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   240→) -> None:
   241→    """Find useEffect calls with empty/whitespace/comment-only bodies."""
   242→    for line_no, line in enumerate(ctx.lines):
   243→        stripped = line.strip()
   244→        if not re.match(r"(?:React\.)?useEffect\s*\(\s*\(\s*\)\s*=>\s*\{", stripped):
   245→            continue
   246→
   247→        text = "\n".join(ctx.lines[line_no : line_no + 30])
   248→        arrow_pos = text.find("=>")
   249→        if arrow_pos == -1:
   250→            continue
   251→        brace_pos = text.find("{", arrow_pos)
   252→        if brace_pos == -1:
   253→            continue
   254→
   255→        body = _extract_block_body(text, brace_pos)
   256→        if body is None:
   257→            continue
   258→
   259→        if _strip_ts_comments(body).strip() == "":
   260→            _emit(smell_counts, "dead_useeffect", ctx, line_no + 1, stripped[:100])
   261→
   262→
   263→def _scan_single_line_chain(
   264→    ctx: _FileContext, index: int, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   265→) -> int:
   266→    """Consume a single-line empty if/else-if chain starting at *index*. Returns next index."""
   267→    cursor = index + 1
   268→    while cursor < len(ctx.lines):
   269→        next_stripped = ctx.lines[cursor].strip()
   270→        if _SINGLE_EMPTY_ELSE_IF.match(next_stripped):
   271→            cursor += 1
   272→            continue
   273→        if _SINGLE_EMPTY_ELSE.match(next_stripped):
   274→            cursor += 1
   275→            continue
   276→        break
   277→    _emit(smell_counts, "empty_if_chain", ctx, index + 1, ctx.lines[index].strip()[:100])
   278→    return cursor
   279→
   280→
   281→def _scan_multi_line_chain(
   282→    ctx: _FileContext, index: int, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   283→) -> int:
   284→    """Consume a multi-line empty if/else chain starting at *index*. Returns next index."""
   285→    chain_all_empty = True
   286→    cursor = index
   287→    while cursor < len(ctx.lines):
   288→        current = ctx.lines[cursor].strip()
   289→        if cursor == index:
   290→            if not _MULTI_IF_OPEN.match(current):
   291→                chain_all_empty = False
   292→                break
   293→        elif _MULTI_ELSE_IF_OPEN.match(current):
   294→            pass
   295→        elif _MULTI_ELSE_OPEN.match(current):
   296→            pass
   297→        elif current == "}":
   298→            tail = cursor + 1
   299→            while tail < len(ctx.lines) and ctx.lines[tail].strip() == "":
   300→                tail += 1
   301→            if tail < len(ctx.lines) and _ELSE_CONT.match(ctx.lines[tail].strip()):
   302→                cursor = tail
   303→                continue
   304→            cursor += 1
   305→            break
   306→        elif current == "":
   307→            cursor += 1
   308→            continue
   309→        else:
   310→            chain_all_empty = False
   311→            break
   312→        cursor += 1
   313→    if chain_all_empty and cursor > index + 1:
   314→        _emit(smell_counts, "empty_if_chain", ctx, index + 1, ctx.lines[index].strip()[:100])
   315→    return max(index + 1, cursor)
   316→
   317→
   318→def _detect_empty_if_chains(
   319→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   320→) -> None:
   321→    """Find if/else chains where all branches are empty."""
   322→    index = 0
   323→    while index < len(ctx.lines):
   324→        stripped = ctx.lines[index].strip()
   325→        if not _IF_START.match(stripped):
   326→            index += 1
   327→            continue
   328→        if _SINGLE_EMPTY_IF.match(stripped):
   329→            index = _scan_single_line_chain(ctx, index, smell_counts)
   330→            continue
   331→        if _MULTI_IF_OPEN.match(stripped):
   332→            index = _scan_multi_line_chain(ctx, index, smell_counts)
   333→            continue
   334→        index += 1
   335→
   336→
   337→def _detect_error_no_throw(
   338→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   339→) -> None:
   340→    """Find console.error calls not followed by throw/return or other handling."""
   341→    # Skip files that are themselves logger/error-handler utilities.
   342→    basename = os.path.basename(ctx.filepath).lower()
   343→    basename_no_ext = os.path.splitext(basename)[0]
   344→    if any(tag in basename_no_ext for tag in _ERROR_HANDLER_BASENAMES):
   345→        return
   346→
   347→    for index, line in enumerate(ctx.lines):
   348→        if "console.error" not in line:
   349→            continue
   350→
   351→        # Skip if preceding 10 lines contain error-boundary or dev-only context.
   352→        preceding = "\n".join(ctx.lines[max(0, index - 10) : index])
   353→        if _PRECEDING_SKIP_PATTERNS.search(preceding):
   354→            continue
   355→
   356→        following = "\n".join(ctx.lines[index + 1 : index + 4])
   357→        if not _HANDLED_RE.search(following):
   358→            _emit(smell_counts, "console_error_no_throw", ctx, index + 1, line.strip()[:100])
   359→
   360→
   361→def _detect_high_cyclomatic_complexity(
   362→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   363→):
   364→    """Flag functions with cyclomatic complexity > 15."""
   365→    for i, line in enumerate(ctx.lines):
   366→        name = _find_function_start(line, ctx.lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
   367→        if not name:
   368→            continue
   369→
   370→        body = _extract_function_body(ctx.lines, i)
   371→        if body is None:
   372→            continue
   373→
   374→        complexity = _compute_ts_cyclomatic_complexity(body)
   375→        if complexity > _HIGH_CYCLOMATIC_THRESHOLD:
   376→            _emit(
   377→                smell_counts, "high_cyclomatic_complexity", ctx, i + 1,
   378→                f"{name}() — cyclomatic complexity {complexity}",
   379→            )
   380→
   381→
   382→def _detect_monster_functions(
   383→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   384→):
   385→    """Find functions/components exceeding 150 LOC via brace-tracking.
   386→
   387→    Matches: function declarations, named arrow functions, and React components.
   388→    Skips: interfaces, types, enums, and objects/arrays.
   389→    """
   390→    for i, line in enumerate(ctx.lines):
   391→        name = _find_function_start(line, ctx.lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
   392→        if not name:
   393→            continue
   394→
   395→        brace_line = _find_opening_brace_line(ctx.lines, i, window=5)
   396→        if brace_line is None:
   397→            continue
   398→
   399→        end_line = _track_brace_body(ctx.lines, brace_line, max_scan=2000)
   400→        if end_line is not None:
   401→            loc = end_line - i + 1
   402→            if loc > _MONSTER_FUNCTION_LOC:
   403→                _emit(
   404→                    smell_counts, "monster_function", ctx, i + 1,
   405→                    f"{name}() — {loc} LOC",
   406→                )
   407→
   408→
   409→def _detect_nested_closures(
   410→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   411→):
   412→    """Find functions with >= 3 nested closure definitions."""
   413→    for i, line in enumerate(ctx.lines):
   414→        name = _find_function_start(line, ctx.lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
   415→        if not name:
   416→            continue
   417→
   418→        body = _extract_function_body(ctx.lines, i)
   419→        if body is None:
   420→            continue
   421→
   422→        closure_count = (
   423→            _count_pattern_in_body(body, _FUNC_RE)
   424→            + _count_pattern_in_body(body, _ARROW_RE)
   425→        )
   426→        if closure_count >= _NESTED_CLOSURE_THRESHOLD:
   427→            _emit(
   428→                smell_counts, "nested_closure", ctx, i + 1,
   429→                f"{name}() — {closure_count} nested closures",
   430→            )
   431→
   432→
   433→def _detect_stub_functions(
   434→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   435→):
   436→    """Find functions with empty body or only return/return null (stub functions).
   437→
   438→    Matches function declarations and named arrow functions.
   439→    Skips decorated functions (TS decorators on line above).
   440→    """
   441→    for i, line in enumerate(ctx.lines):
   442→        if i > 0 and ctx.lines[i - 1].strip().startswith("@"):
   443→            continue
   444→
   445→        name = _find_function_start(line, ctx.lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
   446→        if not name:
   447→            continue
   448→
   449→        body = _extract_function_body(ctx.lines, i, max_scan=30)
   450→        if body is None:
   451→            continue
   452→
   453→        body_clean = _strip_ts_comments(body).strip().rstrip(";")
   454→        if body_clean in ("", "return", "return null", "return undefined"):
   455→            label = body_clean or "empty"
   456→            _emit(
   457→                smell_counts, "stub_function", ctx, i + 1,
   458→                f"{name}() — body is {label}",
   459→            )
   460→
   461→
   462→def _detect_swallowed_errors(
   463→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   464→) -> None:
   465→    """Find catch blocks whose only content is console.error/warn/log."""
   466→    catch_re = re.compile(r"catch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{")
   467→    for match in catch_re.finditer(ctx.content):
   468→        body = _extract_block_body(ctx.content, match.end() - 1, 500)
   469→        if body is None:
   470→            continue
   471→
   472→        body_clean = _strip_ts_comments(body).strip()
   473→        if not body_clean:
   474→            continue
   475→
   476→        statements = [
   477→            stmt.strip().rstrip(";")
   478→            for stmt in re.split(r"[;\n]", body_clean)
   479→            if stmt.strip()
   480→        ]
   481→        if not statements:
   482→            continue
   483→
   484→        all_console = all(
   485→            re.match(r"console\.(error|warn|log)\s*\(", stmt) for stmt in statements
   486→        )
   487→        if all_console:
   488→            line_no, snippet = _content_line_info(ctx.content, match.start())
   489→            _emit(smell_counts, "swallowed_error", ctx, line_no, snippet)
   490→
   491→
   492→def _detect_switch_no_default(
   493→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   494→):
   495→    """Flag switch statements that have no default case."""
   496→    switch_re = re.compile(r"\bswitch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{")
   497→    for m in switch_re.finditer(ctx.content):
   498→        body = _extract_block_body(ctx.content, m.end() - 1, _MAX_SWITCH_BODY_SCAN)
   499→        if body is None:
   500→            continue
   501→
   502→        # Count case labels — only flag if there are actual cases
   503→        case_count = len(re.findall(r"\bcase\s+", body))
   504→        if case_count < _SWITCH_CASE_MINIMUM:
   505→            continue
   506→
   507→        if re.search(r"\bdefault\s*:", body):
   508→            continue
   509→
   510→        line_no, snippet = _content_line_info(ctx.content, m.start())
   511→        _emit(smell_counts, "switch_no_default", ctx, line_no, snippet)
   512→
   513→
   514→def _detect_window_globals(
   515→    ctx: _FileContext, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   516→):
   517→    """Find window.__* assignments — global state escape hatches.
   518→
   519→    Matches:
   520→    - window.__foo = ...
   521→    - (window as any).__foo = ...
   522→    - window['__foo'] = ...
   523→    """
   524→    window_re = re.compile(
   525→        r"""(?:"""
   526→        r"""\(?\s*window\s+as\s+any\s*\)?\s*\.\s*(?:__\w+)"""  # (window as any).__name
   527→        r"""|window\s*\.\s*(?:__\w+)"""  # window.__name
   528→        r"""|window\s*\[\s*['"](?:__\w+)['"]\s*\]"""  # window['__name']
   529→        r""")\s*=""",
   530→    )
   531→    for i, line in enumerate(ctx.lines):
   532→        if i in ctx.line_state:
   533→            continue
   534→        m = window_re.search(line)
   535→        if not m:
   536→            continue
   537→        if _ts_match_is_in_string(line, m.start()):
   538→            continue
   539→        _emit(smell_counts, "window_global", ctx, i + 1, line.strip()[:100])
   540→
```

1→"""Multi-line smell detection utilities (brace-tracked).
     2→
     3→Shared utilities: string-aware scanning, brace tracking, comment stripping,
     4→and line-state classification. All detector functions live in _smell_detectors.py.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from typing import NamedTuple
    10→
    11→from desloppify.base.text_utils import strip_c_style_comments
    12→from desloppify.languages.typescript.syntax.scanner import scan_code
    13→
    14→__all__ = [
    15→    "_FileContext",
    16→    "_build_ts_line_state",
    17→    "_code_text",
    18→    "_content_line_info",
    19→    "_extract_block_body",
    20→    "_find_block_end",
    21→    "_scan_template_content",
    22→    "_strip_ts_comments",
    23→    "_track_brace_body",
    24→    "_ts_match_is_in_string",
    25→    "scan_code",
    26→]
    27→
    28→
    29→class _FileContext(NamedTuple):
    30→    """Per-file data bundle passed to all smell detectors."""
    31→
    32→    filepath: str
    33→    content: str
    34→    lines: list[str]
    35→    line_state: dict[int, str]
    36→
    37→
    38→def _strip_ts_comments(text: str) -> str:
    39→    """Strip // and /* */ comments while preserving strings.
    40→
    41→    Delegates to the shared implementation in utils.py.
    42→    """
    43→    return strip_c_style_comments(text)
    44→
    45→
    46→def _ts_match_is_in_string(line: str, match_start: int) -> bool:
    47→    """Check if a match position falls inside a string literal or comment on a single line.
    48→
    49→    Mirrors Python's _match_is_in_string but for TS syntax (', ", `, //).
    50→    """
    51→    i = 0
    52→    in_str = None
    53→
    54→    while i < len(line):
    55→        if i == match_start:
    56→            return in_str is not None
    57→
    58→        ch = line[i]
    59→
    60→        # Escape sequences inside strings
    61→        if in_str and ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(line):
    62→            i += 2
    63→            continue
    64→
    65→        if in_str:
    66→            if ch == in_str:
    67→                in_str = None
    68→            i += 1
    69→            continue
    70→
    71→        # Line comment — everything after is non-code
    72→        if ch == "/" and i + 1 < len(line) and line[i + 1] == "/":
    73→            return match_start > i
    74→
    75→        if ch in ("'", '"', "`"):
    76→            in_str = ch
    77→            i += 1
    78→            continue
    79→
    80→        i += 1
    81→
    82→    return False
    83→
    84→
    85→def _track_brace_body(
    86→    lines: list[str], start_line: int, *, max_scan: int = 2000
    87→) -> int | None:
    88→    """Find the closing brace that matches the first opening brace from start_line.
    89→
    90→    Tracks brace depth with string-literal awareness (', ", `).
    91→    Returns the line index of the closing brace, or None if not found.
    92→    """
    93→    depth = 0
    94→    found_open = False
    95→    for line_idx in range(start_line, min(start_line + max_scan, len(lines))):
    96→        for _, ch, in_string in scan_code(lines[line_idx]):
    97→            if in_string:
    98→                continue
    99→            if ch == "{":
   100→                depth += 1
   101→                found_open = True
   102→            elif ch == "}":
   103→                depth -= 1
   104→                if found_open and depth == 0:
   105→                    return line_idx
   106→    return None
   107→
   108→
   109→def _find_block_end(content: str, brace_start: int, max_scan: int = 5000) -> int | None:
   110→    """Find the closing brace position in a content string starting from an opening brace.
   111→
   112→    Uses scan_code for string-literal awareness. Returns the index of the
   113→    matching ``}`` or None.
   114→    """
   115→    depth = 0
   116→    for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(
   117→        content, brace_start, min(brace_start + max_scan, len(content))
   118→    ):
   119→        if in_s:
   120→            continue
   121→        if ch == "{":
   122→            depth += 1
   123→        elif ch == "}":
   124→            depth -= 1
   125→            if depth == 0:
   126→                return ci
   127→    return None
   128→
   129→
   130→def _extract_block_body(
   131→    content: str, brace_start: int, max_scan: int = 5000
   132→) -> str | None:
   133→    """Return the text between ``{`` at *brace_start* and its matching ``}``.
   134→
   135→    Delegates to :func:`_find_block_end` for brace tracking.
   136→    Returns ``None`` when the closing brace is not found.
   137→    """
   138→    end = _find_block_end(content, brace_start, max_scan)
   139→    if end is None:
   140→        return None
   141→    return content[brace_start + 1 : end]
   142→
   143→
   144→def _content_line_info(content: str, pos: int) -> tuple[int, str]:
   145→    """Return ``(1-based line number, stripped snippet[:100])`` for a position in *content*."""
   146→    line_no = content[:pos].count("\n") + 1
   147→    line_start = content.rfind("\n", 0, pos) + 1
   148→    line_end = content.find("\n", pos)
   149→    if line_end == -1:
   150→        line_end = len(content)
   151→    return line_no, content[line_start:line_end].strip()[:100]
   152→
   153→
   154→def _code_text(text: str) -> str:
   155→    """Blank string literals and ``//`` comments to spaces, preserving positions.
   156→
   157→    Built on :func:`scan_code` with added line-comment detection.
   158→    """
   159→    out = list(text)
   160→    in_line_comment = False
   161→    prev_code_idx = -2
   162→    prev_code_ch = ""
   163→    for i, ch, in_s in scan_code(text):
   164→        if ch == "\n":
   165→            in_line_comment = False
   166→            prev_code_ch = ""
   167→            continue
   168→        if in_line_comment:
   169→            out[i] = " "
   170→            continue
   171→        if in_s:
   172→            out[i] = " "
   173→            continue
   174→        if ch == "/" and prev_code_ch == "/" and prev_code_idx == i - 1:
   175→            out[prev_code_idx] = " "
   176→            out[i] = " "
   177→            in_line_comment = True
   178→            prev_code_ch = ""
   179→            continue
   180→        prev_code_idx = i
   181→        prev_code_ch = ch
   182→    return "".join(out)
   183→
   184→
   185→def _scan_template_content(
   186→    line: str, start: int, brace_depth: int = 0
   187→) -> tuple[int, bool, int]:
   188→    """Scan template literal content from *start* in *line*.
   189→
   190→    Returns ``(end_pos, found_close, brace_depth)`` where *found_close* is True
   191→    if a closing backtick was found and *end_pos* is the position after it.
   192→    """
   193→    j = start
   194→    while j < len(line):
   195→        ch = line[j]
   196→        if ch == "\\" and j + 1 < len(line):
   197→            j += 2
   198→            continue
   199→        if ch == "$" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "{":
   200→            brace_depth += 1
   201→            j += 2
   202→            continue
   203→        if ch == "}" and brace_depth > 0:
   204→            brace_depth -= 1
   205→            j += 1
   206→            continue
   207→        if ch == "`" and brace_depth == 0:
   208→            return (j + 1, True, brace_depth)
   209→        j += 1
   210→    return (j, False, brace_depth)
   211→
   212→
   213→def _scan_code_line(line: str) -> tuple[bool, bool, int]:
   214→    """Scan a normal code line for block comment or template literal start.
   215→
   216→    Returns ``(entered_block_comment, entered_template, template_brace_depth)``.
   217→    """
   218→    j = 0
   219→    in_str = None
   220→    while j < len(line):
   221→        ch = line[j]
   222→
   223→        # Skip escape sequences
   224→        if in_str and ch == "\\" and j + 1 < len(line):
   225→            j += 2
   226→            continue
   227→
   228→        # String tracking
   229→        if in_str:
   230→            if ch == in_str:
   231→                in_str = None
   232→            j += 1
   233→            continue
   234→
   235→        # Line comment — rest is not code
   236→        if ch == "/" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "/":
   237→            break
   238→
   239→        # Block comment start
   240→        if ch == "/" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "*":
   241→            # Check if it closes on same line
   242→            close = line.find("*/", j + 2)
   243→            if close != -1:
   244→                j = close + 2
   245→                continue
   246→            return (True, False, 0)
   247→
   248→        # Template literal start
   249→        if ch == "`":
   250→            end_pos, found_close, depth = _scan_template_content(line, j + 1)
   251→            if found_close:
   252→                j = end_pos
   253→                continue
   254→            return (False, True, depth)
   255→
   256→        if ch in ("'", '"'):
   257→            in_str = ch
   258→            j += 1
   259→            continue
   260→
   261→        j += 1
   262→
   263→    return (False, False, 0)
   264→
   265→
   266→def _build_ts_line_state(lines: list[str]) -> dict[int, str]:
   267→    """Build a map of line numbers that are inside block comments or template literals.
   268→
   269→    Returns {0-indexed line: reason} where reason is "block_comment" or "template_literal".
   270→    Lines not in the map are normal code lines suitable for regex checks.
   271→
   272→    Tracks:
   273→    - Block comment state (opened by /*, closed by */)
   274→    - Template literal state (opened by backtick, closed by backtick,
   275→      with ${} nesting awareness)
   276→    """
   277→    state: dict[int, str] = {}
   278→    in_block_comment = False
   279→    in_template = False
   280→    template_brace_depth = 0
   281→
   282→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   283→        if in_block_comment:
   284→            state[i] = "block_comment"
   285→            if "*/" in line:
   286→                in_block_comment = False
   287→            continue
   288→
   289→        if in_template:
   290→            state[i] = "template_literal"
   291→            _, found_close, template_brace_depth = _scan_template_content(
   292→                line, 0, template_brace_depth
   293→            )
   294→            if found_close:
   295→                in_template = False
   296→            continue
   297→
   298→        in_block_comment, in_template, depth = _scan_code_line(line)
   299→        if in_template:
   300→            template_brace_depth = depth
   301→
   302→    return state
   303→

1→"""Plan-order and cluster-collapse helpers for work queues."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Any
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.registry import DETECTORS
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.annotations import (
     9→    get_issue_description,
    10→    get_issue_note,
    11→    get_issue_override,
    12→)
    13→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.types import WorkQueueItem
    14→from desloppify.state import StateModel
    15→
    16→
    17→def new_item_ids(state: StateModel) -> set[str]:
    18→    """Return issue IDs added in the most recent scan."""
    19→    scan_history = state.get("scan_history", [])
    20→    if not scan_history:
    21→        return set()
    22→    threshold = scan_history[-1].get("timestamp", "")
    23→    if not threshold:
    24→        return set()
    25→    return {
    26→        issue_id
    27→        for issue_id, issue in state.get("issues", {}).items()
    28→        if issue.get("first_seen", "") >= threshold
    29→    }
    30→
    31→
    32→def enrich_plan_metadata(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> None:
    33→    """Stamp plan description, note, and cluster info from overrides."""
    34→    clusters: dict = plan.get("clusters", {})
    35→
    36→    for item in items:
    37→        issue_id = item["id"]
    38→        description = get_issue_description(plan, issue_id)
    39→        note = get_issue_note(plan, issue_id)
    40→        override = get_issue_override(plan, issue_id)
    41→        if description:
    42→            item["plan_description"] = description
    43→        if note:
    44→            item["plan_note"] = note
    45→        if override.get("cluster"):
    46→            cluster_name = override["cluster"]
    47→            cluster_data = clusters.get(cluster_name, {})
    48→            item["plan_cluster"] = {
    49→                "name": cluster_name,
    50→                "description": cluster_data.get("description"),
    51→                "total_items": len(cluster_data.get("issue_ids", [])),
    52→                "action_steps": cluster_data.get("action_steps") or [],
    53→            }
    54→
    55→
    56→def stamp_plan_sort_keys(
    57→    items: list[WorkQueueItem],
    58→    plan: dict,
    59→    new_ids: set[str],
    60→) -> None:
    61→    """Stamp ``_plan_position`` and ``_is_new`` on each item.
    62→
    63→    These fields are consumed by :func:`item_sort_key` in ``ranking.py``
    64→    to produce the correct final ordering in a single sort pass.
    65→    """
    66→    queue_order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    67→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    68→
    69→    position_map: dict[str, int] = {}
    70→    for idx, issue_id in enumerate(queue_order):
    71→        if issue_id not in skipped_ids:
    72→            position_map[issue_id] = idx
    73→
    74→    for item in items:
    75→        item_id = item["id"]
    76→        pos = position_map.get(item_id)
    77→        item["_plan_position"] = pos  # None if not in queue_order
    78→        item["_is_new"] = item_id in new_ids
    79→
    80→
    81→def separate_skipped(
    82→    items: list[WorkQueueItem],
    83→    plan: dict,
    84→) -> tuple[list[WorkQueueItem], list[WorkQueueItem]]:
    85→    """Separate skipped items from the main list.
    86→
    87→    Returns ``(non_skipped, skipped)`` so callers can optionally re-append.
    88→    """
    89→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    90→    if not skipped_ids:
    91→        return items, []
    92→    non_skipped: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
    93→    skipped: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
    94→    for item in items:
    95→        if item["id"] in skipped_ids:
    96→            skipped.append(item)
    97→        else:
    98→            non_skipped.append(item)
    99→    return non_skipped, skipped
   100→
   101→
   102→def filter_cluster_focus(
   103→    items: list[WorkQueueItem],
   104→    plan: dict,
   105→    cluster: str | None,
   106→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   107→    """Filter to only cluster members when a cluster focus is active."""
   108→    effective_cluster = cluster or plan.get("active_cluster")
   109→    if not effective_cluster:
   110→        return items
   111→    clusters: dict = plan.get("clusters", {})
   112→    cluster_data = clusters.get(effective_cluster, {})
   113→    cluster_member_ids = set(cluster_data.get("issue_ids", []))
   114→    if not cluster_member_ids:
   115→        return items
   116→    return [item for item in items if item["id"] in cluster_member_ids]
   117→
   118→
   119→def stamp_positions(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> None:
   120→    """Stamp queue_position and plan_skipped metadata on each item."""
   121→    skipped_map: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
   122→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(skipped_map.keys())
   123→
   124→    for position, item in enumerate(items):
   125→        item["queue_position"] = position + 1
   126→        if item["id"] in skipped_ids:
   127→            item["plan_skipped"] = True
   128→            skip_entry = skipped_map.get(item["id"])
   129→            if skip_entry:
   130→                item["plan_skip_kind"] = skip_entry.get("kind", "temporary")
   131→                skip_reason = skip_entry.get("reason")
   132→                if skip_reason:
   133→                    item["plan_skip_reason"] = skip_reason
   134→
   135→
   136→def action_type_for_detector(detector: str) -> str:
   137→    """Look up the action_type for a detector from the registry."""
   138→    meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   139→    if meta:
   140→        return meta.action_type
   141→    return "manual_fix"
   142→
   143→
   144→def _build_cluster_meta(
   145→    cluster_name: str, members: list[WorkQueueItem], cluster_data: dict[str, Any]
   146→) -> WorkQueueItem:
   147→    """Build a cluster meta-item from its member items."""
   148→    detector = members[0].get("detector", "") if members else ""
   149→    action = cluster_data.get("action") or ""
   150→    if "desloppify autofix" in action:
   151→        action_type = "auto_fix"
   152→    elif "desloppify move" in action:
   153→        action_type = "reorganize"
   154→    else:
   155→        action_type = action_type_for_detector(detector)
   156→        if action_type == "auto_fix" and "desloppify autofix" not in action:
   157→            action_type = "refactor"
   158→
   159→    stored_desc = cluster_data.get("description") or ""
   160→    total_in_cluster = len(cluster_data.get("issue_ids", []))
   161→    if stored_desc and total_in_cluster != len(members):
   162→        summary = stored_desc.replace(str(total_in_cluster), str(len(members)))
   163→    else:
   164→        summary = stored_desc or f"{len(members)} issues"
   165→
   166→    primary_command = cluster_data.get("action")
   167→    if not primary_command:
   168→        primary_command = f"desloppify next --cluster {cluster_name} --count 10"
   169→
   170→    estimated_impact = max(
   171→        (m.get("estimated_impact", 0.0) for m in members), default=0.0
   172→    )
   173→
   174→    return {
   175→        "id": cluster_name,
   176→        "kind": "cluster",
   177→        "action_type": action_type,
   178→        "summary": summary,
   179→        "members": members,
   180→        "member_count": len(members),
   181→        "primary_command": primary_command,
   182→        "cluster_name": cluster_name,
   183→        "cluster_auto": True,
   184→        "cluster_optional": bool(cluster_data.get("optional")),
   185→        "confidence": "high",
   186→        "detector": detector,
   187→        "file": "",
   188→        "estimated_impact": estimated_impact,
   189→    }
   190→
   191→
   192→def collapse_clusters(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   193→    """Replace cluster member items with single cluster meta-items.
   194→
   195→    Walks the list in order: the first member of each collapsed cluster is
   196→    replaced with its meta-item, subsequent members are skipped.  This
   197→    preserves the ordering established by sort + plan-order.
   198→    """
   199→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   200→    if not clusters:
   201→        return items
   202→
   203→    fid_to_cluster: dict[str, str] = {}
   204→    for name, cluster in clusters.items():
   205→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
   206→            continue
   207→        for issue_id in cluster.get("issue_ids", []):
   208→            fid_to_cluster[issue_id] = name
   209→
   210→    if not fid_to_cluster:
   211→        return items
   212→
   213→    # Collect members per cluster (preserving encounter order)
   214→    cluster_members: dict[str, list[WorkQueueItem]] = {}
   215→    for item in items:
   216→        cname = fid_to_cluster.get(item.get("id", ""))
   217→        if cname:
   218→            cluster_members.setdefault(cname, []).append(item)
   219→
   220→    # Build meta-items only for clusters with 2+ members in the queue
   221→    meta_items: dict[str, WorkQueueItem] = {}
   222→    for cname, members in cluster_members.items():
   223→        if len(members) < 2:
   224→            continue
   225→        meta_items[cname] = _build_cluster_meta(
   226→            cname, members, clusters.get(cname, {})
   227→        )
   228→
   229→    # Walk in order: replace first member of each collapsed cluster
   230→    # with its meta-item, skip subsequent members
   231→    seen_clusters: set[str] = set()
   232→    result: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
   233→    for item in items:
   234→        cname = fid_to_cluster.get(item.get("id", ""))
   235→        if cname and cname in meta_items:
   236→            if cname not in seen_clusters:
   237→                seen_clusters.add(cname)
   238→                result.append(meta_items[cname])
   239→            # skip individual member
   240→        else:
   241→            result.append(item)
   242→    return result
   243→
   244→
   245→__all__ = [
   246→    "collapse_clusters",
   247→    "enrich_plan_metadata",
   248→    "filter_cluster_focus",
   249→    "new_item_ids",
   250→    "separate_skipped",
   251→    "stamp_plan_sort_keys",
   252→    "stamp_positions",
   253→]
   254→

    41→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.types import WorkQueueItem
    42→from desloppify.state import StateModel
    43→
    44→# Sentinel: "read scan_path from state" (the safe default).
    45→# Callers that want to override can pass an explicit str or None.
    46→_SCAN_PATH_FROM_STATE = object()
    47→
    48→
    49→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    50→class QueueBuildOptions:
    51→    """Configuration for queue construction.
    52→
    53→    ``scan_path`` defaults to reading from ``state["scan_path"]`` so callers
    54→    don't need to thread it manually.  Pass an explicit ``str`` or ``None``
    55→    to override (``None`` disables scope filtering).
    56→    """
    57→
    58→    # Output control
    59→    count: int | None = 1
    60→    explain: bool = False
    61→
    62→    # Scope filtering
    63→    scan_path: str | None | object = _SCAN_PATH_FROM_STATE
    64→    scope: str | None = None
    65→    status: str = "open"
    66→    chronic: bool = False
    67→
    68→    # Subjective gating
    69→    include_subjective: bool = True
    70→    subjective_threshold: float = 100.0
    71→
    72→    # Plan integration
    73→    plan: dict | None = None
    74→    include_skipped: bool = False
    75→    cluster: str | None = None
    76→
    77→    # Pre-computed context (overrides plan)
    78→    context: QueueContext | None = None
    79→
    80→
    81→class WorkQueueResult(TypedDict):
    82→    """Typed shape of the dict returned by :func:`build_work_queue`."""
    83→
    84→    items: list[WorkQueueItem]
    85→    total: int
    86→    grouped: dict[str, list[WorkQueueItem]]
    87→    new_ids: set[str]
    88→
    89→
    90→def build_work_queue(
    91→    state: StateModel,
    92→    *,
    93→    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
    94→) -> WorkQueueResult:
    95→    """Build a ranked work queue from state issues.
    96→
    97→    Pipeline:
    98→    1. Gather    — issue items, subjective dimensions, workflow stages
    99→    2. Score     — estimate impact from dimension headroom, apply floor
   100→    3. Presort   — stamp plan positions, separate skipped items
   101→    4. Lifecycle — filter endgame-only items when objective work remains
   102→    5. Sort      — rank by impact/confidence, apply plan order
   103→    6. Limit     — truncate to count, optionally add explain metadata
   104→    """
   105→    opts = options or QueueBuildOptions()
   106→    plan, scan_path, status, threshold = _resolve_inputs(opts, state)
   107→
   108→    # 1. Gather
   109→    items = build_issue_items(
   110→        state, scan_path=scan_path, status_filter=status,
   111→        scope=opts.scope, chronic=opts.chronic,
   112→    )
   113→    items += _gather_subjective_items(state, opts, threshold)
   114→    items += _gather_workflow_items(state, plan, status)
   115→
   116→    # 2. Score & filter
   117→    enrich_with_impact(items, state.get("dimension_scores", {}))
   118→    items = [i for i in items if _passes_impact_floor(i)]
   119→
   120→    # 3. Plan-aware ordering (part 1: separate skipped items)
   121→    new_ids, skipped = _plan_presort(items, state, plan)
   122→
   123→    # 4. Lifecycle filter — endgame-only items filtered when objective work remains
   124→    items = _apply_lifecycle_filter(items)
   125→
   126→    # 5. Sort & plan post-processing
   127→    items.sort(key=item_sort_key)
   128→    _plan_postsort(items, skipped, plan, opts)
   129→
   130→    # 6. Finalize
   131→    if not items:
   132→        items += _empty_queue_fallback(plan)
   133→    total = len(items)
   134→    if opts.count is not None and opts.count > 0:
   135→        items = items[:opts.count]
   136→    if opts.explain:
   137→        for item in items:
   138→            item["explain"] = item_explain(item)
   139→
   140→    return {
   141→        "items": items,
   142→        "total": total,
   143→        "grouped": group_queue_items(items, "item"),
   144→        "new_ids": new_ids,
   145→    }
   146→
   147→
   148→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   149→# Pipeline helpers (private to this module)
   150→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   151→
   152→
   153→def _resolve_inputs(
   154→    opts: QueueBuildOptions, state: StateModel,
   155→) -> tuple[dict | None, str | None, str, float]:
   156→    """Resolve plan, scan_path, status, and subjective threshold from options."""
   157→    ctx = opts.context
   158→    plan = ctx.plan if ctx is not None else opts.plan
   159→
   160→    scan_path: str | None = (
   161→        state.get("scan_path")
   162→        if opts.scan_path is _SCAN_PATH_FROM_STATE
   163→        else opts.scan_path  # type: ignore[assignment]
   164→    )
   165→
   166→    status = opts.status
   167→    if status not in ALL_STATUSES:
   168→        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported status filter: {status}")
   169→
   170→    try:
   171→        threshold = float(opts.subjective_threshold)
   172→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   173→        threshold = 100.0
   174→    threshold = max(0.0, min(100.0, threshold))
   175→
   176→    return plan, scan_path, status, threshold
   177→
   178→
   179→def _gather_subjective_items(
   180→    state: StateModel,
   181→    opts: QueueBuildOptions,
   182→    threshold: float,
   183→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   184→    """Build subjective dimension candidates.
   185→
   186→    Lifecycle filtering (endgame gating) happens in _apply_lifecycle_filter,
   187→    not here. This function only handles configuration and scope.
   188→    """
   189→    if not opts.include_subjective:
   190→        return []
   191→    if opts.status not in {"open", "all"}:
   192→        return []
   193→    if opts.chronic:
   194→        return []
   195→
   196→    candidates = build_subjective_items(
   197→        state, state.get("issues", {}), threshold=threshold,
   198→    )
   199→    return [item for item in candidates if scope_matches(item, opts.scope)]
   200→
   201→
   202→def _gather_workflow_items(
   203→    state: StateModel, plan: dict | None, status: str,
   204→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   205→    """Inject triage stages, checkpoints, and create-plan when plan is active."""
   206→    if not plan or status not in {"open", "all"}:
   207→        return []
   208→
   209→    items: list[WorkQueueItem] = list(build_triage_stage_items(plan, state))
   210→    for builder in (
   211→        build_score_checkpoint_item,
   212→        build_import_scores_item,
   213→        build_communicate_score_item,
   214→    ):
   215→        item = builder(plan, state)
   216→        if item is not None:
   217→            items.append(item)
   218→    plan_item = build_create_plan_item(plan)
   219→    if plan_item is not None:
   220→        items.append(plan_item)
   221→    return items
   222→
   223→
   224→_MIN_STANDALONE_IMPACT = 0.05
   225→
   226→
   227→def _passes_impact_floor(item: WorkQueueItem) -> bool:
   228→    """Return True if item should survive the impact floor filter."""
   229→    if item.get("kind") != "issue":
   230→        return True
   231→    if item.get("is_review") or item.get("is_subjective"):
   232→        return True
   233→    impact = item.get("estimated_impact")
   234→    return not impact or impact >= _MIN_STANDALONE_IMPACT
   235→
   236→
   237→def _plan_presort(
   238→    items: list[WorkQueueItem], state: StateModel, plan: dict | None,
   239→) -> tuple[set[str], list[WorkQueueItem]]:
   240→    """Enrich plan metadata and stamp sort keys before sorting.
   241→
   242→    Returns ``(new_ids, skipped)`` — skipped items are removed from
   243→    ``items`` in place and returned separately for post-sort re-append.
   244→    """
   245→    if not plan:
   246→        return set(), []
   247→
   248→    new_ids = _new_item_ids(state)
   249→    enrich_plan_metadata(items, plan)
   250→    stamp_plan_sort_keys(items, plan, new_ids)
   251→    remaining, skipped = separate_skipped(items, plan)
   252→    items[:] = remaining
   253→    return new_ids, skipped
   254→
   255→
   256→def _plan_postsort(
   257→    items: list[WorkQueueItem],
   258→    skipped: list[WorkQueueItem],
   259→    plan: dict | None,
   260→    opts: QueueBuildOptions,
   261→) -> None:
   262→    """Re-append skipped items, stamp positions, filter to cluster focus."""
   263→    if not plan:
   264→        return
   265→
   266→    if opts.include_skipped:
   267→        items.extend(skipped)
   268→    stamp_positions(items, plan)
   269→    focused = filter_cluster_focus(items, plan, opts.cluster)
   270→    items[:] = focused
   271→
   272→
   273→def _has_objective_items(items: list[WorkQueueItem]) -> bool:
   274→    """True if any objective mechanical work items remain in the queue."""
   275→    return any(
   276→        i.get("kind") == "issue"
   277→        and i.get("detector", "") not in NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS
   278→        for i in items
   279→    )
   280→
   281→
   282→def _is_endgame_only(item: WorkQueueItem) -> bool:
   283→    """True if this item should only appear when the objective queue is drained."""
   284→    return (
   285→        item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension"
   286→        and not item.get("initial_review")
   287→    )
   288→
   289→
   290→def _apply_lifecycle_filter(items: list[WorkQueueItem]) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   291→    """Enforce lifecycle visibility rules.
   292→
   293→    Endgame-only items (subjective reassessment) are filtered out when
   294→    objective mechanical work remains. Initial review items always survive
   295→    — they're onboarding priority, not endgame work.
   296→    """
   297→    if not _has_objective_items(items):
   298→        return items  # endgame: everything visible
   299→    return [i for i in items if not _is_endgame_only(i)]
   300→
   301→
   302→def _empty_queue_fallback(plan: dict | None) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   303→    """Return a 'run scan' nudge when an active plan cycle has cleared."""
   304→    if not plan:
   305→        return []
   306→    plan_scores = plan.get("plan_start_scores", {})
   307→    if plan_scores.get("strict") is None:
   308→        return []
   309→    return [{
   310→        "id": "workflow::run-scan",
   311→        "kind": "workflow_action",
   312→        "summary": "Queue cleared \u2014 run scan to finalize and reveal your updated score.",
   313→        "primary_command": "desloppify scan",
   314→        "file": "",
   315→        "detector": "workflow",
   316→        "confidence": "high",
   317→    }]
   318→
   319→

   324→    "WorkQueueResult",
   325→    "build_work_queue",
   326→    "collapse_clusters",
   327→    "group_queue_items",
   328→]

1→"""Queue mutation helpers for plan operations."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.promoted_ids import add_promoted_ids
     6→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel, SkipEntry, ensure_plan_defaults
     7→
     8→
     9→def _remove_id_from_lists(plan: PlanModel, issue_id: str) -> None:
    10→    """Remove a issue ID from queue_order and skipped."""
    11→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
    12→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
    13→    if issue_id in order:
    14→        order.remove(issue_id)
    15→    skipped.pop(issue_id, None)
    16→
    17→
    18→def _resolve_position(
    19→    order: list[str],
    20→    position: str,
    21→    target: str | None = None,
    22→    offset: int | None = None,
    23→    issue_ids: list[str] | None = None,
    24→) -> int:
    25→    """Resolve a position specifier to an insertion index.
    26→
    27→    *issue_ids* are the IDs being moved — used to calculate relative
    28→    positions when they already exist in the list.
    29→    """
    30→    moving = set(issue_ids or [])
    31→
    32→    if position == "top":
    33→        return 0
    34→    if position == "bottom":
    35→        return len(order)
    36→
    37→    if position in {"before", "after"} and target:
    38→        return _resolve_relative_position(order, moving, position=position, target=target)
    39→
    40→    if position in {"up", "down"} and offset is not None:
    41→        return _resolve_offset_position(
    42→            order,
    43→            moving,
    44→            position=position,
    45→            offset=offset,
    46→            issue_ids=issue_ids,
    47→        )
    48→
    49→    return len(order)
    50→
    51→
    52→def _resolve_relative_position(
    53→    order: list[str],
    54→    moving: set[str],
    55→    *,
    56→    position: str,
    57→    target: str,
    58→) -> int:
    59→    for i, item_id in enumerate(order):
    60→        if item_id == target and item_id not in moving:
    61→            return i if position == "before" else i + 1
    62→    return 0 if position == "before" else len(order)
    63→
    64→
    65→def _find_index(items: list[str], target: str) -> int | None:
    66→    for i, item_id in enumerate(items):
    67→        if item_id == target:
    68→            return i
    69→    return None
    70→
    71→
    72→def _resolve_offset_position(
    73→    order: list[str],
    74→    moving: set[str],
    75→    *,
    76→    position: str,
    77→    offset: int,
    78→    issue_ids: list[str] | None,
    79→) -> int:
    80→    if not issue_ids:
    81→        return 0 if position == "up" else len(order)
    82→    first_id = issue_ids[0]
    83→    clean_order = [x for x in order if x not in moving]
    84→    current_idx = _find_index(clean_order, first_id)
    85→    if current_idx is None:
    86→        if position == "up":
    87→            return max(0, len(clean_order) - offset)
    88→        return len(clean_order)
    89→    if position == "up":
    90→        return max(0, current_idx - offset)
    91→    return min(len(clean_order), current_idx + offset)
    92→
    93→
    94→def move_items(
    95→    plan: PlanModel,
    96→    issue_ids: list[str],
    97→    position: str,
    98→    target: str | None = None,
    99→    offset: int | None = None,
   100→) -> int:
   101→    """Move issue IDs to a position in queue_order. Returns count moved."""
   102→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   103→
   104→    # Triage stage IDs are workflow-managed and cannot be manually reordered
   105→    from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import TRIAGE_IDS
   106→
   107→    issue_ids = [fid for fid in issue_ids if fid not in TRIAGE_IDS]
   108→    if not issue_ids:
   109→        return 0
   110→
   111→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   112→
   113→    # Remove from skipped if present
   114→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
   115→    for fid in issue_ids:
   116→        skipped.pop(fid, None)
   117→
   118→    # Remove from current position in order
   119→    for fid in issue_ids:
   120→        if fid in order:
   121→            order.remove(fid)
   122→
   123→    # Resolve insertion point
   124→    idx = _resolve_position(order, position, target, offset, issue_ids)
   125→
   126→    # Insert in original order
   127→    for i, fid in enumerate(issue_ids):
   128→        order.insert(idx + i, fid)
   129→
   130→    # Track user-promoted IDs only when explicitly moved to the front.
   131→    # Moving to bottom/down/after should not create a promotion barrier.
   132→    if position == "top":
   133→        add_promoted_ids(plan, issue_ids)
   134→
   135→    return len(issue_ids)
   136→
   137→
   138→__all__ = ["move_items"]
   139→
```

1→"""Pure policy helpers for stale/unscored subjective planning decisions."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import hashlib
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.config import DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
     8→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
     9→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import slugify
    10→from desloppify.engine.planning.scorecard_projection import all_subjective_entries
    11→
    12→_REVIEW_DETECTORS = ("review", "concerns")
    13→
    14→
    15→def current_stale_ids(
    16→    state: StateModel,
    17→    *,
    18→    subjective_prefix: str = "subjective::",
    19→) -> set[str]:
    20→    """Return ``subjective::<slug>`` IDs that are currently stale."""
    21→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {}) or {}
    22→    if not dim_scores:
    23→        return set()
    24→
    25→    stale: set[str] = set()
    26→    for entry in all_subjective_entries(state, dim_scores=dim_scores):
    27→        if not entry.get("stale"):
    28→            continue
    29→        dim_key = entry.get("dimension_key", "")
    30→        if dim_key:
    31→            stale.add(f"{subjective_prefix}{slugify(dim_key)}")
    32→    return stale
    33→
    34→
    35→def current_unscored_ids(
    36→    state: StateModel,
    37→    *,
    38→    subjective_prefix: str = "subjective::",
    39→) -> set[str]:
    40→    """Return ``subjective::<slug>`` IDs that are currently unscored."""
    41→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    42→    if isinstance(assessments, dict) and assessments:
    43→        unscored: set[str] = set()
    44→        for dim_key, payload in assessments.items():
    45→            if not isinstance(payload, dict):
    46→                continue
    47→            if not payload.get("placeholder"):
    48→                continue
    49→            if dim_key:
    50→                unscored.add(f"{subjective_prefix}{slugify(dim_key)}")
    51→        return unscored
    52→
    53→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {}) or {}
    54→    if not dim_scores:
    55→        return set()
    56→
    57→    unscored = set()
    58→    for data in dim_scores.values():
    59→        if not isinstance(data, dict):
    60→            continue
    61→        detectors = data.get("detectors", {})
    62→        meta = detectors.get("subjective_assessment")
    63→        if not isinstance(meta, dict):
    64→            continue
    65→        if not meta.get("placeholder"):
    66→            continue
    67→        dim_key = meta.get("dimension_key", "")
    68→        if dim_key:
    69→            unscored.add(f"{subjective_prefix}{slugify(dim_key)}")
    70→    return unscored
    71→
    72→
    73→def current_under_target_ids(
    74→    state: StateModel,
    75→    *,
    76→    target_strict: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE,
    77→    subjective_prefix: str = "subjective::",
    78→) -> set[str]:
    79→    """Return under-target subjective IDs that are neither stale nor unscored."""
    80→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {}) or {}
    81→    if not dim_scores:
    82→        return set()
    83→
    84→    stale_ids = current_stale_ids(state, subjective_prefix=subjective_prefix)
    85→    unscored_ids = current_unscored_ids(state, subjective_prefix=subjective_prefix)
    86→
    87→    under_target: set[str] = set()
    88→    for entry in all_subjective_entries(state, dim_scores=dim_scores):
    89→        if entry.get("placeholder") or entry.get("stale"):
    90→            continue
    91→        strict_val = float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))
    92→        if strict_val >= target_strict:
    93→            continue
    94→        dim_key = entry.get("dimension_key", "")
    95→        if not dim_key:
    96→            continue
    97→        item_id = f"{subjective_prefix}{slugify(dim_key)}"
    98→        if item_id not in stale_ids and item_id not in unscored_ids:
    99→            under_target.add(item_id)
   100→    return under_target
   101→
   102→
   103→def review_issue_snapshot_hash(state: StateModel) -> str:
   104→    """Hash open review/concerns issue IDs to detect triage-relevant changes."""
   105→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
   106→    review_ids = sorted(
   107→        issue_id
   108→        for issue_id, issue in issues.items()
   109→        if issue.get("status") == "open" and issue.get("detector") in _REVIEW_DETECTORS
   110→    )
   111→    if not review_ids:
   112→        return ""
   113→    return hashlib.sha256("|".join(review_ids).encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
   114→
   115→
   116→def compute_new_issue_ids(plan: dict, state: StateModel) -> set[str]:
   117→    """Return open review/concerns IDs that appeared since the last triage."""
   118→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
   119→    triaged = set(meta.get("triaged_ids", []))
   120→    current = {
   121→        issue_id
   122→        for issue_id, issue in state.get("issues", {}).items()
   123→        if issue.get("status") == "open" and issue.get("detector") in _REVIEW_DETECTORS
   124→    }
   125→    return current - triaged if triaged else set()
   126→
   127→
   128→def is_triage_stale(
   129→    plan: dict,
   130→    state: StateModel,
   131→    *,
   132→    triage_ids: set[str] | frozenset[str] = frozenset(),
   133→) -> bool:
   134→    """Return True when triage should run because review work has changed."""
   135→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
   136→
   137→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
   138→    current_review_ids = {
   139→        issue_id
   140→        for issue_id, issue in issues.items()
   141→        if issue.get("status") == "open" and issue.get("detector") in _REVIEW_DETECTORS
   142→    }
   143→    triaged_ids = set(meta.get("triaged_ids", []))
   144→    new_since_triage = current_review_ids - triaged_ids
   145→    if new_since_triage:
   146→        return True
   147→
   148→    confirmed = set(meta.get("triage_stages", {}).keys())
   149→    if confirmed:
   150→        order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   151→        if order & set(triage_ids):
   152→            return True
   153→    return False
   154→
   155→
   156→__all__ = [
   157→    "compute_new_issue_ids",
   158→    "current_stale_ids",
   159→    "current_under_target_ids",
   160→    "current_unscored_ids",
   161→    "is_triage_stale",
   162→    "review_issue_snapshot_hash",
   163→]
   164→
